 2. A.   11.11.14 
2. RECOGNITION 
A. TSD Mission Moment – Leonard Elementary School “Peer to 
Peer Mentoring” 
Last year, Lisa Carruthers, teacher at Leonard Elementary School,  worked 
tirelessly to implement the “Peer to Peer Mediation” program into her third 
grade classroom.  Peer to Peer Mediation is an evidence -based practice 
that positively impacts students with Autism Spectrum Disorders as well as 
other students with disabi lities. In essence, the Peer to Peer program 
develops peer relationships.  Students learn about autism and how it 
affects other students in their classroom.  Mrs. Carruthers attended 
seminars during the summer months and met with other teachers that 
had implemented the program.  Mrs. Carruthers is currently busy sharing 
her knowledge with other teachers at Leonard and around the Troy School 
District. 
Lisa Car ruthers will be recognized and asked to come  up to receive a 
certificate. 
3
     2. B.   11.11.14 
2.   RECOGNITION  
B.   NUMATS Outstanding Educator Award 
Each year,  Northwestern University’s Midwest Academic Talent Search 
recognizes educators from across the country for their e nthusiastic 
participation in a special program  which provides gifted students the 
opportunity to take assessments designed for older students. NUMATS, as it 
is called, is used to determine placement in high school courses, and the top 
scoring students are honored in a special ceremony . NUMATS also 
recognizes the important role that educators play in the support and 
implementation of the program. In 2014, only 18 educators from the United 
States were singled out for extraordinary recognition . We are very pleased 
tonight to acknowledge  Tracey Kinney, TSD  Assessment Coordinator, for 
receiving this prestigious recognition.  Congratulations, Tracey! 
________________________________________________ 
Recommended Action:  Present certificate.

Superintendent’s Student 
Superintendent’s Student Advisory Board was instituted 
this year for the purpose of providing action-oriented 
leadership opportunities to st udents at the high school 
level while also serving as a sounding-board to the 
Superintendent and Central Office administration.  
Students will serve on this board throughout their high 
school career – graduating seniors will be replaced by a 
new group of freshmen at the beginning of each school 
year.  We received a total of 55 applications submitted by 
outstanding students from Athens, International 
Academy-East, Niles and Troy High Schools. Out of these 
applications, 24 students were selected; 6 seniors, 7 
juniors, 7 sophomores and 4 freshmen.   Congratulations 
to the following students who have been asked to serve 
as the Superintendent’s Advisory Board:     Alexzander 
Dudek, Curtis Hsu, Arsha Ali,  Mara Quinn, Hannah Schell, 
Emily Skwarek, Goutham Karuppiah, Katerina Levy, Ben 
Mansky, Emma Thomas, Hars h Patel, Meghan Romig, 
Scott Mittelbrun, Afriti Chinoy, Esha Ghosalkar, Keegan 
McInerney, David Nguyen, Thejas Suvarna, Rida Farook, 
Peggy Randon, Shreya Shettigar, Maheem Syed, Jeremy 
Huotari, and Armondo Jalou.  Dr. Machesky shared that 
the first meeting for the advisory board will be held on

Mrs. Anne Mull provided the Board with an overview of 
both teachers and the administrative staff.  She talked 
about the August Professional Development sessions that 
were held for teachers and th e topics that were covered 
and shared that a visit was made to the Detroit Institute 
of Arts and the Troy Nature Center.  She also talked 
about the areas of focus for professional development 
opportunities that will be of fered throughout the year. 
She provided an update on Focus Schools and shared the 
Title 1 school-wide plans for the upcoming school year.  
She talked about District initiatives, collaboration, 
mentoring and having conversations about growth.  She 
also mentioned that TSD hosted a Peer District Meeting 
this afternoon. 
There was additional discu ssion about Peer District 
Meetings and the benefits of networking and sharing 
information with similar districts.  
Placement in the District.  He explained that on an annual 
basis, the number and percentage of students 
participating in the high school advanced placement 
programs are tracked as they help to clarify the number 
of graduates taking advantage of advanced placement 
classes as well as scoring three or higher on the exam. 
He shared that TSD students have exceeded both the 
state and national average pass rate on Advanced 
Placement Exams by more than 20%.  He also stated 
that there are more students participating in AP Exams 
compared to several years ago.   He stated that students 
who take AP courses in high school are more successful 
when they leave high school and are prepared for college 
and careers.  He also indica ted that students passing AP 
Exams receive college credit, which saves in tuition costs.  
He reviewed the levels of distinction - AP Scholar, AP 
Scholar with Honor, AP Scho lar with Distinction, and 
National AP Scholar and shared the number of students 
who received scores in those levels of distinction.

would be flexible.

         6. B. 11.11.14 
6. B.  PERSONNEL 
CONSIDERATION:  Administrative Appointment –  
     Assistant Superintendent, Business Services 
I am pleased to recommend the appointment of Mr. Rick West to the position 
of Assistant Superintendent, Business Services.  Mr. West comes to Troy 
from Royal Oak Schools , where he spent the past 3 years serving as the 
Executive Director of Finance and Operations.  Prior to Royal Oak Schools, Mr. 
West spent 4 years as the Business Manager for Atherton Community Schools. In 
addition, Mr. West is currently the President of the Oakland County School 
Business Officials and a member of the Michigan School Business Officials 
Professional Development Committee. He received his Masters in Business 
Administration from Texas A  & M and his Bachelors in Business Administration 
from the University of Michigan - Flint.  
With your approval, Mr. Rick West  will begin his duties as Assistant 
Superintendent, Business Services , effective December 1, 2014 , or as soon as 
possible thereafter.  
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Acceptance of the resolution below:  
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by         
 Supported by        
BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Board of Education issues a two-year 
probationary administrative contract without tenure in the administrative position 
to Mr. Rick West, who will begin his duties as Assistant Superintendent, Business 
Services, effective December 1, 2014, or as soon as possible thereafter.

BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED that the Troy School District’s Board of

271
     8. A.        11.11.14 
TROY SCHOOL DISTRICT 
TROY, MICHIGAN 48098 
ANNUAL SUMMER TAX RESOLUTION 
 WHEREAS, this Board of Ed ucation by resolution of November 11, 2014, 
determined to impose a summer property tax levy to collect one -half of school property 
taxes, including debt services, upon property located within the Troy School District, 
beginning with 20 15 and continuing from year to year until specifically revoked by this 
Board of Education. 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1.  This Board of Education, pursuant to 1976 P.A. 451, as amended, hereby 
invokes a summer tax levy of one -half of school property taxes, including d ebt service, 
continuing with 2015, and continuing from year to year until specifically revoked by this 
Board of Education, and requests each city in which this district is located and in which a 
local school district or city is concurrently imposing a summer tax levy to collect those 
summer taxes. 
 2.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is authorized and directed to 
forward to the governing body of each city in which this district is located and in which a 
local school district or city is concurrently imposing a summer tax levy, a copy of this 
Board’s resolution requesting that each such city agree to coll ect the summer tax levy 
for 2015 in the amount spe cified in this resolution.  Such forwarding of the resolution 
and the request to collect the summer tax levy shall be sent so that they are received by 
the appropriate governing bodies before January 1, 2015. 
 3.  The Superintendent or his/her designee is  authorized and directed to 
negotiate on behalf of this District with the governing body of each city in which the 
District is located for the reasonable expenses for collection of the District’s summer tax 
levy that the city may bill under MCLA 380.1612.  Any such proposed agreement shall be 
brought before this Board for its approval or disapproval. 
 4.  All resolutions and parts of resolutions insofar as they conflict with the 
provisions of this resolution be and the same are hereby rescinded. 
 Yes: Members 
 No: Members 
Resolution declared adopted.       _____________________________ 
  Secretary, Board of Education 
 The undersigned, duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of 
Education of Troy School District, Troy, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing 
constitutes a true and complete copy of a resolution adopted by said Board of Education 
at a regular meeting held on November 11, 2014, the original of which is part of the 
Board’s minutes.  The undersigned further certifies that notice of the meeting was given 
to the public pursuant to the provisions of the “Open Meetings Act” (Act 267, P .A. 1976, 
as amended).
